My health has been pretty stable, honestly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes, I do have trouble finishing things, even when I know exactly what needs to be done. It's really just at the clinic where I'm... well, where I feel like I'm hitting a wall.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless, fidgety, or have trouble staying seated during the day, especially at work?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. It's just becoming a real problem for me to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you find it difficult to stay focused or organized—like during conversations, reading, or managing personal tasks?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being much of a problem at home, to be honest. It's really just at the clinic where I'm... well, where I feel like I'm hitting a wall.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless, fidgety, or have trouble staying seated during the day, especially at work?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. It's really just at the clinic where I'm... well, where I feel like I'm hitting a wall.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless, fidgety, or have trouble staying seated during the day, especially at work?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I don't really have any trouble sitting still. Um, I can stay at my desk for hours if I need to. It's really just my mind that wanders off. But physically... no, I don't feel restless or fidgety at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't really feel depressed or... down at all. I still enjoy doing the things I normally do outside of work, and my mood is fine, honestly. It's really just the frustration with my focus at the clinic that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these issues with your focus? Were they always present, or is this something that developed more recently?\n\nPatient: It's definitely more recent. Um, I'd say it really started after my promotion, which was about two years and four months ago. Before that, I managed fine. But with the promotion came a much larger portfolio, and... well, it just started overwhelming the systems I used to rely on to keep everything straight.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on a task at work, do you find it difficult to prioritize steps, hold multiple pieces of information in your mind at once, or shift your thinking when needed?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My problem isn't figuring out the steps or juggling the details in my head, it's just... actually keeping my attention on the task itself once I sit down to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical changes, medical issues, or started any new medications around the time these focus problems began?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't had any new medical issues or physical changes, and I didn't start any new medications back then, either. My health has been pretty stable, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to get started on tasks at work, or do you often put things off until the last minute?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely put things off. I find myself delaying the dense policy reviews and long data-analysis blocks until late in the day. The thought of just sitting down with that much detailed reading and thinking... it feels almost physically hard to start. So I just keep finding other things to do instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any difficulty starting or finishing tasks, even outside of work, that you weren’t aware of before—like managing bills, following through on chores, or staying focused during activities that require sustained attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do have trouble finishing things, even when I know exactly what needs to be done. Like, if someone gives me three or four follow-up steps after a committee meeting, I'll usually do the first one right away. I might even start drafting the second one. But then... the email or the spreadsheet or the final submission just sits there. I'll just set something down and immediately lose track of it. I haven't really noticed that happening at home. I mean, I can usually keep track of my keys and my phone just fine, and I don't have any trouble remembering what I need to do around the house. It really just seems to be an issue when I'm at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself easily distracted by things around you, even in everyday situations like watching TV, having a conversation, or listening to music—where you have to refocus your attention more often than before?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. If I'm just watching a show or talking with someone at home, I'm fine. I don't really find myself drifting off or getting distracted by things around me in those situations. I can focus on a conversation or listen to music without having to force my attention back to it. Like I mentioned, all of this really just seems to hit me when I'm at the clinic.\n\nDoctor: When you're struggling to focus at work and things start to pile up, do you find yourself getting unusually frustrated, impatient, or overwhelmed compared to how you used to handle stress?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, managing a clinic is always going to have its stressful moments, but I handle it about the same as I always have. I don't find myself getting unusually frustrated or losing my patience with my staff or anything like that. It's really just the annoyance of misplacing my things, but I don't feel emotionally overwhelmed by the stress itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself struggling to stay focused or finish tasks that require you to sit down and concentrate for a while, even in settings outside of work—like when working on a hobby, studying, or managing personal projects?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Outside of work, I don't have those kinds of problems. If I sit down to pay bills or read a book, I can get through it just fine. It's really just at the clinic where I can't seem to stay on track. Um, I'd say it really started after my promotion, which was about two years and four months ago. Before that, I managed fine. But with the promotion came a much larger portfolio, and... well, it just started overwhelming the systems I used to rely on to keep everything straight.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on a task at work, do you find it difficult to prioritize steps, hold multiple pieces of information in your mind at once, or shift your thinking when needed?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have trouble prioritizing what needs to be done, or holding onto information. And shifting gears is fine—if something urgent comes up at the clinic, I can pivot to it without an issue. My problem isn't figuring out the steps or juggling the details in my head, it's just... actually keeping my attention on the task itself once I sit down to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical changes, medical issues, or started any new medications around the time these focus problems began?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't had any new medical issues or physical changes, and I didn't start any new medications back then, either. My health has been pretty stable, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to get started on tasks at work, or do you often put things off until the last minute?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely put things off. I find myself delaying the dense policy reviews and long data-analysis blocks until late in the day. The thought of just sitting down with that much detailed reading and thinking... it feels almost physically hard to start. So I just keep finding other things to do instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any difficulty starting or finishing tasks, even outside of work, that you weren’t aware of before—like managing bills, following through on chores, or staying focused during activities that require sustained attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do have trouble finishing things, even when I know exactly what needs to be done. Like, if someone gives me three or four follow-up steps after a committee meeting, I'll usually do the first one right away. I might even start drafting the second one. But then... the email or the spreadsheet or the final submission just sits there. It stays unfinished until someone actually follows up and reminds me about it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you find yourself avoiding tasks that require sustained mental effort, like filling out forms, planning events, or working on detailed projects at home?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I haven't noticed myself avoiding those kinds of things at home. Doing personal paperwork, filling out forms, or planning things in my personal life... it doesn't seem to bother me the way my work tasks do. I usually just get them done without much of a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you often lose track of important items, like keys or documents, or find yourself disorganized when managing tasks in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I'm constantly hunting for things at work. My badge, my handwritten notes, clinic folders... or even the flash drive I literally just had in my hand right before a meeting. I'll just set something down and immediately lose track of it. It’s really embarrassing, honestly, because I used to be the organized one.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself losing track of personal items or forgetting steps in tasks at home, even during routine activities like grocery shopping or preparing meals?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed that happening at home. I mean, I can usually keep track of my keys and my phone just fine, and I don't have any trouble remembering what I need to do around the house. It really just seems to be an issue when I'm at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself easily distracted by things around you, even in everyday situations like watching TV, having a conversation, or listening to music—where you have to refocus your attention more often than before?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. If I'm just watching a show or talking with someone at home, I'm fine. I don't really find myself drifting off or getting distracted by things around me in those situations. I can focus on a conversation or listen to music without having to force my attention back to it. Like I mentioned, all of this really just seems to hit me when I'm at the clinic.\n\nDoctor: When you're struggling to focus at work and things start to pile up, do you find yourself getting unusually frustrated, impatient, or overwhelmed compared to how you used to handle stress?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, managing a clinic is always going to have its stressful moments, but I handle it about the same as I always have. I don't find myself getting unusually frustrated or losing my patience with my staff or anything like that. It's really just the annoyance of misplacing my things, but I don't feel emotionally overwhelmed by the stress itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself struggling to stay focused or finish tasks that require you to sit down and concentrate for a while, even in settings outside of work—like when working on a hobby, studying, or managing personal projects?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Outside of work, I don't have those kinds of problems. If I sit down to pay bills or read a book, I can get through it just fine. It's really just at the clinic where I can't seem to stay on track. At home, my focus is normal.\n\nDoctor: When you're under pressure or dealing with more demanding tasks at home, like organizing something important or handling a sudden responsibility, do you find it harder to focus or stay on top of things the way you do at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have that problem at all. Um, if something sudden comes up at home or I need to organize something big, I just handle it. I don't lose my focus or drop the ball. It really is just like I've been saying—everything is completely fine until I step foot in the clinic. At home, even if it's demanding, I stay on top of things without any issue.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I find myself delaying the dense policy reviews and long data-analysis blocks until late in the day. The thought of just sitting down with that much detailed reading and thinking... it feels almost physically hard to start. But then... the email or the spreadsheet or the final submission just sits there. I haven't noticed myself avoiding those kinds of things at home. Doing personal paperwork, filling out forms, or planning things in my personal life... it doesn't seem to bother me the way my work tasks do. So I just keep finding other things to do instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any difficulty starting or finishing tasks, even outside of work, that you weren’t aware of before—like managing bills, following through on chores, or staying focused during activities that require sustained attention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do have trouble finishing things, even when I know exactly what needs to be done. Like I mentioned, all of this really just seems to hit me when I'm at the clinic.\n\nDoctor: When you're struggling to focus at work and things start to pile up, do you find yourself getting unusually frustrated, impatient, or overwhelmed compared to how you used to handle stress?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. It's really just the annoyance of misplacing my things, but I don't feel emotionally overwhelmed by the stress itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself struggling to stay focused or finish tasks that require you to sit down and concentrate for a while, even in settings outside of work—like when working on a hobby, studying, or managing personal projects?\n\nPatient: No, not really. At home, my focus is normal.\n\nDoctor: When you're under pressure or dealing with more demanding tasks at home, like organizing something important or handling a sudden responsibility, do you find it harder to focus or stay on top of things the way you do at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have that problem at all. Um, if something sudden comes up at home or I need to organize something big, I just handle it. I don't lose my focus or drop the ball. It really is just like I've been saying—everything is completely fine until I step foot in the clinic. At home, even if it's demanding, I stay on top of things without any issue.\n\nDoctor: When you're in less structured environments, like on vacation or during days off when there’s no pressing schedule, do you find it harder to stay focused or get tasks done compared to when you're in a more routine or structured setting?\n\nPatient: No, not at all.
